# Manual steps and what is not migrated

Generated by StackToIT from `execution-plan.json`. Nothing in this folder has been run: StackToIT reads and writes files and creates, changes and deletes nothing in any cloud. Every step is carried out by a person, by OpenTofu or by a script of this folder that a person runs. Paths are relative to the output folder.

## Manual steps

The 288 manual steps of the plan in 48 work packages, in the order of the Work packages table of `execution/README.md`. Each package says who carries it out, what it touches, what it waits for, how far it is and when it is done; its steps follow it in full, folded. Steps that say the same thing on different resources are one entry that gives the text once, with {resource} for the resource, and lists each step by its name beside its resource; what every step of an entry comes after is said once, and so is the text a line of each step begins and ends with, with its field in braces for what each step says there.

### `WP-01` — Answer the decisions the plan asks as steps (prepare)

- Owner: Network Architect
- Steps: 1 (1 requires a design decision)
- Affected resources: `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private`
- Rests on: `DEC-NET-STACKITROUTE-NAT-FIXED-EGRESS-ADDRESS-LOST`
- Waits for: `DEC-NET-STACKITROUTE-NAT-FIXED-EGRESS-ADDRESS-LOST`

- Done when: the decision DEC-NET-STACKITROUTE-NAT-FIXED-EGRESS-ADDRESS-LOST is answered (accepted or rejected, with its evidence) in the inputs of the run and is no longer open when the run is repeated

<details>
<summary>The 1 manual step of WP-01</summary>

#### The decision step of module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private (`DEC/routing/DEC-NET-STACKITROUTE-NAT-FIXED-EGRESS-ADDRESS-LOST`)

- Phase: Prepare
- What: Answer decision DEC-NET-STACKITROUTE-NAT-FIXED-EGRESS-ADDRESS-LOST: Decide the egress address of module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private: keep the changing address of the STACKIT network, or give each server that an allow-list names a STACKIT public IP
- Why: a NAT gateway's fixed egress address is lost on STACKIT: the address a routed network leaves by is the network's own, shared by every routed network of the project, not fixed and not settable
- Who: Network Architect
- When: before pilot
- Needs: the answer to the decision DEC-NET-STACKITROUTE-NAT-FIXED-EGRESS-ADDRESS-LOST
- Done when: the decision DEC-NET-STACKITROUTE-NAT-FIXED-EGRESS-ADDRESS-LOST is answered (accepted or rejected, with its evidence) in the inputs of the run and is no longer open when the run is repeated
- Source: `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private`
- State: not executable — requires a design decision — blocked by `DEC-NET-STACKITROUTE-NAT-FIXED-EGRESS-ADDRESS-LOST`
- Rollback (manual): the plan does not know how this step is undone: the person who carries it out records how

### `WP-03` — Carry the routing the draft does not write (prepare)

- Owner: Network Architect
- Steps: 2 (2 a person can start now)
- Affected resources: `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.public`
- State: a person can start it now
- Done when: each step meets the condition stated with it below (2 different conditions).

<details>
<summary>The 2 manual steps of WP-03</summary>

#### Alike steps on 2 resources (aws_route_table) — the routing precondition of {resource} (`MAN/routing/precondition-outside-area:{resource}`)

- Phase: Prepare
- What: Create the target project outside a Network Area: {resource} is replaced by STACKIT's default routing, which was observed to give a routed network the same reachability only in a project outside a Network Area
- Who: Network Architect
- When: before pilot
- Needs: the STACKIT project the draft is created in (var.project_id) and whether it lies inside a Network Area
- Done when: the target project exists outside a Network Area, or the architect has decided how {resource} is replaced inside the area
- State: executable: a person can start it now
- Rollback (manual): the plan does not know how this step is undone: the person who carries it out records how

The steps, each by its name, with the resource that stands for {resource} above, its target objects, the steps it comes after besides those above and the lines that are its own (for a line above with a placeholder in braces, what stands for it):

- The routing precondition of module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private (`MAN/routing/precondition-outside-area:module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private`) — {resource} = `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private`
  - Why: STACKIT's default routing was observed to give a routed network the same reachability only in a project outside a Network Area (tested on STACKIT in eu01 on 2026-10-10); the default routing table of an area was never read (the organisation read answered 403), so a project created inside an existing area (var.project_id naming one) is not covered by this replacement and the table needs the architect's decision there
- The routing precondition of module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.public (`MAN/routing/precondition-outside-area:module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.public`) — {resource} = `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.public`
  - Why: STACKIT's default routing was observed to give a routed network the same reachability only in a project outside a Network Area (tested on STACKIT in eu01 on 2026-10-10); the default routing table of an area was never read (the organisation read answered 403), so a project created inside an existing area (var.project_id naming one) is not covered by this replacement and the table needs the architect's decision there. Also not equivalent for a server WITHOUT a public IP: the source's internet gateway serves only an instance with a public IPv4 or Elastic IP address, so such a server had no internet path, while on STACKIT it leaves through the network's shared SNAT address (tested on STACKIT on 2026-10-10): that egress is new, the table does not carry it, and it is decided on the subnet (subnet-egress-ipv4-unproven)

</details>

### `WP-05` — Routing architecture review (foundation)

- Owner: Network Architect
- Steps: 2 (2 a person can start now)
- Affected resources: `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private`, `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.public`
- State: a person can start it now
- Done when: each step meets the condition stated with it below (2 different conditions).

<details>
<summary>The 2 manual steps of WP-05</summary>

#### Alike steps on 2 resources (aws_route_table) — the review of the open settings of {resource} (`MAN/semantic/{resource}/open-settings`)

- Phase: Foundation
- Who: Network Architect
- State: executable: a person can start it now
- Rollback (manual): the plan does not know how this step is undone: the person who carries it out records how

The steps, each by its name, with the resource that stands for {resource} above, its target objects, the steps it comes after besides those above and the lines that are its own (for a line above with a placeholder in braces, what stands for it):

- The review of the open settings of module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private (`MAN/semantic/module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private/open-settings`) — {resource} = `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private`
  - What: Review the 7 open critical settings of `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private` (aws_route_table) that the draft does not carry, and decide for each what the STACKIT target does: set it up by hand, or record that production does not need it (`routes`, `route-destinations`, `route-targets`, `nat-fixed-egress-address`, `egress-restriction`, `subnet-association-target`, `tcr-route-table-vpc-id`)
  - Why: The assessment leaves 7 critical settings of `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_route_table.private` open and the draft does not carry them: a person decides what the target does for each. Each setting states its own reason with its item.
  - When: applies only if its count (local.create_private_subnets && local.max_subnet_length > 0 ? local.nat_gateway_count : 0) holds when OpenTofu plans — decided by data OpenTofu reads when it plans (module.vpc.data.aws_availability_zones.available); not known from the source
  - Needs: the answer to each decision register record (decisions.json): what the STACKIT target does for the setting, or the accepted difference - `routes` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE, Network Architect); `route-destinations` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE, Network Architect); `route-targets` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE, Network Architect); `nat-fixed-egress-address` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE, Network Architect); `egress-restriction` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE, Network Architect); `subnet-association-target` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE, Network Architect); `tcr-route-table-vpc-id` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE, Network Architect)
  - Done when: every one of the 7 settings is closed: each is set up on its STACKIT target and checked, or the decision that production does not need it is recorded (the condition of each is stated with its item, and `assurance/critical-semantics.json` says what closes it)
  - Settings (7, the step is done when every one is closed):
    - `routes` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. Custom routes map to stackit_routing_table_route, an experimental resource (experiment routing-tables); next hops that name AWS gateways are chosen anew.
      - When: applies only if its count (local.create_private_subnets && local.max_subnet_length > 0 ? local.nat_gateway_count : 0) holds when OpenTofu plans — decided by data OpenTofu reads when it plans (module.vpc.data.aws_availability_zones.available); not known from the source
      - Done when: A person creates the routes on the STACKIT routing table (stackit_routing_table_route, experimental) and chooses the next hops anew, because they name AWS gateways.
    - `route-destinations` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. Destinations are read by value: every route of a default-only table sends 0.0.0.0/0 to an internet or NAT gateway — STACKIT's default network routing, observed equivalent for a draft outside a Network Area (the test of 2026-10-10) and review all the same.
      - When: applies only if its count (local.create_private_subnets && local.max_subnet_length > 0 ? local.nat_gateway_count : 0) holds when OpenTofu plans — decided by data OpenTofu reads when it plans (module.vpc.data.aws_availability_zones.available); not known from the source
      - Done when: A person confirms every route destination that is not the default route to an internet or NAT gateway (a CIDR, a prefix list, any IPv6 route): the draft carries none, and a specific destination needs a stackit_routing_table_route, which takes IPv4 CIDR destinations only.
    - `route-targets` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The next hop of each route of the table: a route whose target is anything but an internet or NAT gateway, or that names none, makes the table a custom routing table.
      - When: applies only if its count (local.create_private_subnets && local.max_subnet_length > 0 ? local.nat_gateway_count : 0) holds when OpenTofu plans — decided by data OpenTofu reads when it plans (module.vpc.data.aws_availability_zones.available); not known from the source
      - Done when: A person decides the next hop of each route on STACKIT: an internet gateway becomes the next hop internet, an appliance (a network interface or an instance) a next hop of type ipv4. A NAT gateway, a transit gateway or a VPC peering has no STACKIT counterpart, so the connectivity design is the person's.
    - `nat-fixed-egress-address` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. NOT CARRIED, an observed loss (the test of 2026-10-10): the NAT gateway's fixed egress address.
      - When: applies only if its count (local.create_private_subnets && local.max_subnet_length > 0 ? local.nat_gateway_count : 0) holds when OpenTofu plans — decided by data OpenTofu reads when it plans (module.vpc.data.aws_availability_zones.available); not known from the source
      - Done when: A person accepts the project's shared egress address, which can change and cannot be set, or gives each server a STACKIT public IP when a third party allow-lists the old address.
    - `egress-restriction` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-ROUTE-TABLE-PRIVATE)

A person decides which networks of the Network Area take the routing table and sets routing_table_id on them: STACKIT attaches a routing table to a whole network (stackit_network.routing_table_id), there is no per-subnet association, and routing tables apply only to routed Layer 3 networks in a Network Area.

#### The setting `public-address` of module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_eip.nat (`MAN/semantic/module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_eip.nat/public-address`)

- Phase: Foundation
- What: Decide what the STACKIT target of `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_eip.nat` (aws_eip) does for its critical setting `public-address`, which the draft does not carry, and set it up by hand, or record that production does not need it
- Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The address changes. An Elastic IP of a NAT gateway is its fixed egress address, an open question on STACKIT (fixed egress ip of the NAT gateway rule).
- Who: Network Architect
- When: Timing to be scheduled by the owner
- Needs: the answer to the decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-EIP-NAT (decisions.json): what the STACKIT target does for the critical setting `public-address`, or the accepted difference
- Done when: A person decides how peers that allow-list the Elastic IP are served: STACKIT egress uses the project's shared address, which differs from the Elastic IP, cannot be set and can change; a STACKIT public IP per server keeps its address.
- Source: `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_eip.nat`
- State: executable: a person can start it now
- Risk: the assessment leaves the critical setting `public-address` of `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_eip.nat` open (REVIEW: a person decides what the target does for it): the migration of `module.vpc.module.vpc.aws_eip.nat` is not complete until this step is done
- Rollback (manual): the plan does not know how this step is undone: the person who carries it out records how

    - Done when: A person decides how peers that allow-list the Elastic IP are served: a routed STACKIT network leaves by the project's shared egress address, which cannot be set and can change, so the allow-list follows it or each server gets a STACKIT public IP.

    - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. A zonal NAT gateway (subnet_id) is placed in one AZ; a regional one lists its per-AZ egress in availability_zone_address(es).availability_zone. STACKIT has no NAT gateway resource and one outbound path serves every zone, so a per-zone egress has no counterpart, as for the zones of an Azure NAT gateway.
    - Done when: A person decides whether one egress path for every zone is acceptable where the source keeps one NAT gateway per zone (for zone independence or per-zone addresses), or accepts the difference; a source with one zonal NAT gateway for every subnet loses nothing in placement.

  - Needs ({needs}): `default-nacl-rules` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-VPC-THIS, Network Architect); `default-route-table` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-VPC-THIS, Network Architect); `default-security-group-omitted-direction` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-VPC-THIS, Network Architect); `main-table-set` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-VPC-THIS, Network Architect)
  - Done when ({done when}): 4
  - Settings (4, the step is done when every one is closed):
    - `default-nacl-rules` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-VPC-THIS)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The account's default network ACL is adopted by the VPC (aws_default_network_acl); a deny rule, a rule that is not all-protocol, a CIDR other than 0.0.0.0/0 or ::/0, or an explicitly empty rule list narrows or leaves undecided what the AWS default allow-all states.
      - Done when: A person re-expresses the default network ACL's rules as security group rules, because STACKIT has no stateless subnet ACL; no STACKIT documentation states that a server or network without an attached security group allows all traffic.
    - `default-route-table` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-VPC-THIS)
      - Why: A check of the knowledge base (vpc main table undecided) fired on this unit and leaves the setting to a person: the target does not settle it by itself (a finding of this unit describes it). The account's default route table is adopted (a facet of the VPC, aws_default_route_table, AWS provider 6.66.0). Routes other than a plain 0.0.0.0/0 to an internet or NAT gateway are left for review.
      - Done when: A person decides what this finding asks, and records the evidence or the decision. The finding says: Which table the VPC's subnets follow without an association of their own, and so what their routing and exposure are, is not decided here; the draft attaches no routing table through the main table.
    - `default-security-group-omitted-direction` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-VPC-THIS)
      - Why: A check of the knowledge base (vpc default sg no ingress rules) fired on this unit and leaves the setting to a person: the target does not settle it by itself (a finding of this unit describes it). The adopted default security group of the VPC (a facet, aws_default_security_group) does not state an ingress or an egress direction. Nothing is drafted for this facet, so the empty direction is not reproduced: a person decides whether servers that used the default group must stay without outbound traffic (delete the allow-all egress rules of the STACKIT group after apply) or are meant to have it, and puts them in a STACKIT group that states no ingress rule, not in the project's default group.
      - Done when: A person decides what this finding asks, and records the evidence or the decision. The finding says: The adopted default security group states no ingress rule. The AWS provider removes every rule of the default group when it adopts it and creates only the stated ones, so after apply the group allows no inbound traffic at all, not even from the members of the group itself. Nothing is drafted for the adopted group: decide which servers relied on the default group and attach them to a STACKIT group that states no ingress rule, not to the project's default group, whose own-member rules would admit its members.
    - `main-table-set` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-VPC-MODULE-VPC-AWS-VPC-THIS)
      - Why: A check of the knowledge base (vpc main table undecided) fired on this unit and leaves the setting to a person: the target does not settle it by itself (a finding of this unit describes it). The setting names the route table that is the main route table of the VPC, when the configuration sets it or the scan cannot tell. STACKIT has no main route table.
      - Done when: A person decides what this finding asks, and records the evidence or the decision. The finding says: Which table the VPC's subnets follow without an association of their own, and so what their routing and exposure are, is not decided here; the draft attaches no routing table through the main table.

#### Alike steps on 2 resources (aws_subnet) — the setting `availability-zone` of {resource} (`MAN/semantic/{resource}/availability-zone`)

- Phase: Foundation
- What: Decide what the STACKIT target of `{resource}` (aws_subnet) does for its critical setting `availability-zone`, which the draft does not carry, and set it up by hand, or record that production does not need it
- Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The availability zone of the subnet. STACKIT networks are regional (stackit_network has no zone field, pinned provider); the zone is chosen per server and volume, and AWS zone names do not map to STACKIT zones.
- Who: Platform Architect
- Done when: A person decides, per source zone, which STACKIT zone (eu01-1, eu01-2 or eu01-3) takes the servers and volumes that sat in it, so that the source's spread and its failure domains are kept.
- State: executable: a person can start it now
- Risk: the assessment leaves the critical setting `availability-zone` of `{resource}` open (REVIEW: a person decides what the target does for it): the migration of `{resource}` is not complete until this step is done
- Rollback (manual): the plan does not know how this step is undone: the person who carries it out records how

#### The setting `deletion-window` of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_kms_key.cmk (`MAN/semantic/module.retail_app_ecs.aws_kms_key.cmk/deletion-window`)

- Phase: Security
- What: Decide what the STACKIT target of `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_kms_key.cmk` (aws_kms_key) does for its critical setting `deletion-window`, which the draft does not carry, and set it up by hand, or record that production does not need it
- Why: A check of the knowledge base (kms deletion window) fired on this unit and leaves the setting to a person: the target does not settle it by itself (a finding of this unit describes it). STACKIT KMS deletes a key scheduled for deletion after 30 days (docs create-and-manage-keys, 'Delete a key'), with no setting: 30 is preserved, another window review.
- Who: Identity Architect
- When: Timing to be scheduled by the owner
- Needs: the answer to the decision register record DEC-IAM-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-AWS-KMS-KEY-CMK (decisions.json): what the STACKIT target does for the critical setting `deletion-window`, or the accepted difference
- Done when: A person decides what this finding asks, and records the evidence or the decision. The finding says: The key's deletion window is not 30 days. STACKIT KMS deletes a key scheduled for deletion after 30 days, with no setting (stackit_kms_key has none) — check that a different recovery window is acceptable.
- Source: `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_kms_key.cmk`

      - Why: This draft writes no stackit_security_group, stackit_security_group_rule for this unit: every unit that names the group is a managed data service of STACKIT, which has no security group and takes its clients from an access list of its own, so the group is listed by no resource and what it admitted reaches those access lists as addresses only. What follows states the knowledge base's candidate target, not a block of this draft: Not enforced: the security group is attached to no block of the target (it is written in the draft and listed by no resource, or, where the access lists of its members carry it, not written), so its rules ("security group rules") restrict nothing on the target until an attachment is designed; this setting is not carried as an enforced control. Unsupported, by name: a STACKIT security group has no network (VPC) scope. stackit_security_group takes project_id, name, description, labels, stateful and region only (pinned provider), and the IaaS API object SecurityGroup has no network field either (stackit-api-specifications services/iaas/v2); the group is project-wide and can be attached to the network interfaces of every network of the project.
      - Done when: A person designs how the target enforces what the group allowed (attaches the group, or its replacement, to the target resources that take its place), or accepts the difference.
    - `stateful` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-CATALOG-OPENSEARCH-SG)
      - Why: This draft writes no stackit_security_group, stackit_security_group_rule for this unit: every unit that names the group is a managed data service of STACKIT, which has no security group and takes its clients from an access list of its own, so the group is listed by no resource and what it admitted reaches those access lists as addresses only. What follows states the knowledge base's candidate target, not a block of this draft: Not enforced: the security group is attached to no block of the target (it is written in the draft and listed by no resource, or, where the access lists of its members carry it, not written), so its rules ("security group rules") restrict nothing on the target until an attachment is designed; this setting is not carried as an enforced control. STACKIT security groups are stateful by default (tested on a STACKIT project on 2026-09-24: stateful=true).
      - Done when: A person designs how the target enforces what the group allowed (attaches the group, or its replacement, to the target resources that take its place), or accepts the difference.
    - `egress-restriction` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-CATALOG-OPENSEARCH-SG)
      - Why: A check of the knowledge base (sg egress restricted) fired on this unit and leaves the setting to a person: the target does not settle it by itself (a finding of this unit describes it). The security group restricts its outbound traffic: it has no rule that allows every protocol to 0.0.0.0/0 or ::/0.
      - Done when: A person decides what this finding asks, and records the evidence or the decision. The finding says: The source restricts egress (the AWS provider removes AWS's default allow-all egress rule; only the listed egress rules apply).
    - `standalone-rule-not-generated` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-CATALOG-OPENSEARCH-SG)

What follows states the knowledge base's candidate target, not a block of this draft: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. An aws_security_group_rule the draft does not generate (the draft names its settings NOT CARRIED, or the KB cannot decide that it generates it): a prefix list, an ICMP type with every code, ICMPv6 limited by type or code, a protocol with no STACKIT counterpart, a source or protocol or port not visible statically, a source group that is not one whole reference to a security group of the draft, or a repeated or conditionally created group. A person re-creates the rule with the STACKIT range or group it needs (a rule is never widened: a stackit_security_group_rule without source allows every address, pinned provider), per rule.

  - Why: The assessment leaves 4 critical settings of `module.dependencies.aws_security_group.mq` open and the draft does not carry them: a person decides what the target does for each. Each setting states its own reason with its item.
  - Needs ({needs}): (record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-MQ, Network Architect); `stateful` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-MQ, Network Architect); `egress-restriction` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-MQ, Network Architect); `standalone-rule-not-generated` (record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-MQ, Network Architect)
  - Done when ({done when}): 4
  - Settings (4, the step is done when every one is closed):
    - `security-group-vpc-membership` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-MQ)
      - Why: This draft writes no stackit_security_group, stackit_security_group_rule for this unit: every unit that names the group is a managed data service of STACKIT, which has no security group and takes its clients from an access list of its own, so the group is listed by no resource and what it admitted reaches those access lists as addresses only. What follows states the knowledge base's candidate target, not a block of this draft: Not enforced: the security group is attached to no block of the target (it is written in the draft and listed by no resource, or, where the access lists of its members carry it, not written), so its rules ("security group rules") restrict nothing on the target until an attachment is designed; this setting is not carried as an enforced control. Unsupported, by name: a STACKIT security group has no network (VPC) scope. stackit_security_group takes project_id, name, description, labels, stateful and region only (pinned provider), and the IaaS API object SecurityGroup has no network field either (stackit-api-specifications services/iaas/v2); the group is project-wide and can be attached to the network interfaces of every network of the project.
      - Done when: A person designs how the target enforces what the group allowed (attaches the group, or its replacement, to the target resources that take its place), or accepts the difference.
    - `stateful` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-MQ)
      - Why: This draft writes no stackit_security_group, stackit_security_group_rule for this unit: every unit that names the group is a managed data service of STACKIT, which has no security group and takes its clients from an access list of its own, so the group is listed by no resource and what it admitted reaches those access lists as addresses only. What follows states the knowledge base's candidate target, not a block of this draft: Not enforced: the security group is attached to no block of the target (it is written in the draft and listed by no resource, or, where the access lists of its members carry it, not written), so its rules ("security group rules") restrict nothing on the target until an attachment is designed; this setting is not carried as an enforced control. STACKIT security groups are stateful by default (tested on a STACKIT project on 2026-09-24: stateful=true).
      - Done when: A person designs how the target enforces what the group allowed (attaches the group, or its replacement, to the target resources that take its place), or accepts the difference.
    - `egress-restriction` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-MQ)
      - Why: A check of the knowledge base (sg egress restricted) fired on this unit and leaves the setting to a person: the target does not settle it by itself (a finding of this unit describes it). The security group restricts its outbound traffic: it has no rule that allows every protocol to 0.0.0.0/0 or ::/0.
      - Done when: A person decides what this finding asks, and records the evidence or the decision. The finding says: The source restricts egress (the AWS provider removes AWS's default allow-all egress rule; only the listed egress rules apply).
    - `standalone-rule-not-generated` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-SECURITY-GROUP-MQ)
      - Why: This draft writes no stackit_security_group, stackit_security_group_rule for this unit: every unit that names the group is a managed data service of STACKIT, which has no security group and takes its clients from an access list of its own, so the group is listed by no resource and what it admitted reaches those access lists as addresses only. What follows states the knowledge base's candidate target, not a block of this draft: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. An aws_security_group_rule the draft does not generate (the draft names its settings NOT CARRIED, or the KB cannot decide that it generates it): a prefix list, an ICMP type with every code, ICMPv6 limited by type or code, a protocol with no STACKIT counterpart, a source or protocol or port not visible statically, a source group that is not one whole reference to a security group of the draft, or a repeated or conditionally created group. A person re-creates the rule with the STACKIT range or group it needs (a rule is never widened: a stackit_security_group_rule without source allows every address, pinned provider), per rule.
      - Done when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.

- Phase: Data services
- What: Close what the plan cannot decide yet, from the move host, with read-only commands; no password goes on a command line or into the history of the shell. Source (`valkey-cli -h \<source-host> -p \<source-port>`): `INFO server` (the engine and its version), `INFO keyspace` and `DBSIZE` (the keys and the keys with an expiry; on every primary when cluster mode is on, listed with `CLUSTER NODES`), `INFO memory` (used_memory and maxmemory), `SCAN 0 COUNT 100` with `TYPE`, `DUMP` and `PTTL` of a few keys of each family (the commands of the copy; the types the copy has no branch for show here), and the credential the copy signs in with. Target (after the draft is applied and the instance and a service key exist; `valkey-cli -h \<target-host> -p \<target-port> --tls --sni \<target-host> --user \<target-user> --askpass`, where `--askpass` makes the client ask for the password of the service key at its prompt): `INFO server` (Valkey 8), `DBSIZE` (0) and `INFO memory`; do not expect `CONFIG`, `ACL` or `MODULE`, which a probe found denied to the service key. Check that the move host is in `parameters.sgw_acl` of the instance (remove it afterwards), reaches both ends, and that the clients of the cache are listed with the TLS and the credential each uses. Runbook section: `terraform/handover/cache-dependencies_checkout_elasticache_redis_default/data-move.md#preflight`.

### `WP-20` — Set up the log delivery and its labels (platform services)

- Owner: Platform Architect
- Steps: 3 (1 waits for a manual prerequisite, 1 waits for an automated step, 1 requires a design decision)
- Affected resources: `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks`
- Target: `stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d`
- Rests on: `DEC-OPS-STACKITLOGS-RETENTION-OVER-180-DAYS`
- Waits for: `DEC-OPS-STACKITLOGS-RETENTION-OVER-180-DAYS`
- State: waits for decisions (1 of 3 manual steps)
- Done when: each step meets the condition stated with it below (3 different conditions).

<details>
<summary>The 3 manual steps of WP-20</summary>

#### The step "Configure a cold-storage log flow for…" (`MAN/logs/archive:module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks`)

- Phase: Platform services
- What: Configure a cold-storage log flow for module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks: send a copy of its logs, in parallel to the Logs instance stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d, to a long-term store (for example a separate Object Storage bucket) that keeps them for the retention beyond 180 days
- Why: the source keeps its logs for ever and a STACKIT Logs instance keeps at most 180 days (retention beyond 180 days is not supported by the managed service): the first 180 days are kept in the Logs instance, and the longer retention is an archive outside it, which STACKIT recommends as a cold-storage approach (a stream of the log data to a low-cost long-term store in parallel to Logs). The plan creates no archive store, configures no stream and sets no retention of the archive. The step exists only when the decision about the logs beyond 180 days chooses the archive; where the requirement is more than 180 days inside STACKIT Logs with no external archive, the archive is rejected and the migration cannot meet that requirement with STACKIT Logs alone
- Who: Platform Architect
- When: cutover prerequisite
- Needs: where the archive is kept (for example an Object Storage bucket) and the retention in days the workload needs there (the source keeps its logs for ever)
- Done when: a line the log producers of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks send is found both in the Logs instance and in the archive, and logs older than 180 days are readable from the archive
- Source: `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks`
- After: the OpenTofu step for stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d
- State: not executable — requires a design decision — blocked by `DEC-OPS-STACKITLOGS-RETENTION-OVER-180-DAYS`; and, as the plan states: the risks listed below
- Risk: this step holds only once DEC-OPS-STACKITLOGS-RETENTION-OVER-180-DAYS is answered: it is of the path archive-longer-retention-elsewhere of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks, not of a path the draft carries out by default
- Rollback (available): stop the stream to the archive and delete the archive store; the Logs instance and the logs in it are not changed

#### The step "Before any log source writes to stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d" (`MAN/logs/token:stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d`)

- Phase: Platform services
- What: Before any log source writes to stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d: create a write access token for the instance (stackit_logs_access_token with the permission write), and point the shipper at the instance's ingest_url (Loki API) or ingest_otlp_url (OpenTelemetry) from an address var.logs_acl_retention_180d admits
- Why: the plan generates no access token: the token is a credential written to the state, its lifetime (unset, it never expires) and its permissions are choices the source does not state, and one token belongs to one instance; the access list is var.logs_acl_retention_180d, a required input with no default the person gives before the instance is planned: what an instance with no ACL entry allows is not established, so the plan neither leaves it out nor accepts an empty list
- Who: Platform Architect
- When: cutover prerequisite
- Needs: No input is named for this step
- Done when: a write access token for stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d exists, and a log line sent with the token to the ingest address of the instance, from an address var.logs_acl_retention_180d admits, is accepted
- Source: `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks`
- Target: `stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d`
- After: the OpenTofu step for stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d
- State: executable once the automated step it comes after has run: it waits for the OpenTofu step for stackit_logs_instance.logs_retention_180d
- Rollback (manual): the plan does not know how this step is undone: the person who carries it out records how

#### The step "Choose a static label and send the name of…" (`MAN/logs/label-convention:module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks`)

- Phase: Platform services
- What: Choose a static label and send the name of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks (and of its streams) from its log producers as the value of that label
- Why: STACKIT Logs has no log group or stream object that can hold a name, only labels the shipper sends (no-log-group-or-stream-object); the plan chooses no label key and reconfigures no producer
- Who: Platform Architect
- When: cutover prerequisite
- Needs: No input is named for this step
- Done when: a static label is chosen, the log producers of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks send its name as the value of that label, and a line one of them sends is found by that label
- Source: `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_tasks`

Who may reach the load balancer, read from the ingress rules of the security groups that filter its clients. A group reference, a prefix list, an IPv6 range or a variable is not carried: the plan lists the clients the target would block and adds the client-access manual step for the Security Architect.
      - Done when: A person decides it as that finding asks, and the finding's closing evidence or decision is recorded. A person confirms the carried ranges against the source rules, or decides the open client-access step, and the regenerated draft carries the result.
    - `request-handling` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-ALB-AWS-LB-THIS)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The setting states how the load balancer treats HTTP requests that may carry a request-smuggling (desync) risk, and how it rewrites the X-Forwarded-For header before the target sees it.
      - Done when: A person confirms that the application does not depend on either behaviour: the STACKIT application load balancer has neither setting and its documentation does not state the behaviour (pinned provider), also for the AWS default values.
    - `lb-routing-and-addressing` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-ALB-AWS-LB-THIS)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The setting states how the load balancer answers DNS for clients (Availability Zone affinity), whether it still forwards to targets when AWS WAF cannot be reached (fail open), zonal shift, customer-owned address pools and IPv6 source NAT prefixes.
      - Done when: A person decides whether the STACKIT load balancer needs an equivalent for each of them, because the STACKIT application and network load balancers have no setting for any of them (pinned provider).
    - `lb-subnets` (Network Architect; decision register record DEC-NET-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-ALB-AWS-LB-THIS)
      - Why: The value is not visible statically (a case of the mapping rule for this kind of unit), so nothing is credited for it. The load balancer takes the network of its one converted subnet (networks.network_id; several subnets are lb subnets several: STACKIT networks are regional, one network holds what the source spread over zonal subnets.
      - Done when: A literal value or a resolved reference in the source closes it, or a person confirms the value.

The STACKIT application load balancer has no such attribute and the draft does not carry them: a person decides whether the applications behind the load balancer (long polling, streaming, WebSockets, slow requests) cope with the STACKIT default. aws_alb, the alias type, is not read here.

      - Done when: A person translates the task definition into Kubernetes Deployments and Services on SKE (CPU and memory, environment, secrets and health checks become manifest fields); the rule names a Cloud Foundry push as the other way for a simple web app.

The draft writes it as the replicas of the Deployment in kubernetes/\<service>/manifests.yaml when it is a literal number (a HorizontalPodAutoscaler owns the count where the service has a scaling target and policy the draft translates; a number that is not visible statically is a REQUIRED_ token) — a person confirms the number.
      - Done when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.

The draft turns it into the cpu request and limit of the container where it writes the manifest; the sizing against the node pools stays a decision.
      - Done when: A person sets the equivalent on the SKE workload (resource requests and limits, ephemeral storage limit, node pool placement) and records the value the replacement uses, or that it does not need it.

The draft turns it into the memory request and limit of the container where it writes the manifest; the sizing against the node pools stays a decision.
      - Done when: A person sets the equivalent on the SKE workload (resource requests and limits, ephemeral storage limit, node pool placement) and records the value the replacement uses, or that it does not need it.

      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. aws_ecs_task_definition.requires_compatibilities (workload compatibility) (a facet of the unit): the launch types the task is validated against, EC2, EXTERNAL, FARGATE or MANAGED_INSTANCES (provider docs ecs_task_definition.html.markdown:237), which decides where the task can run and which task-level settings are allowed. (knowledge-base evidence, not in this folder)
      - Done when: A person sets the equivalent on the SKE workload (resource requests and limits, ephemeral storage limit, node pool placement) and records the value the replacement uses, or that it does not need it.
    - `twin-ecs-service--launch-type` (Platform Architect; decision register record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CARTS-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SERVICE-THIS)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. aws_ecs_service.launch_type (workload compatibility): the capacity the tasks run on, EC2, FARGATE or EXTERNAL (default EC2; provider docs ecs_service.html.markdown:251), which decides the isolation of a task, its sizing steps and where it can run. Nothing of it is carried: the draft rewrites the service as a Deployment that runs on an SKE node pool and stackit_ske_cluster has no argument for it (the unit names it NOT CARRIED), so a person decides the placement on the target. (knowledge-base evidence, not in this folder)
      - Done when: A person decides where the pods of the service run on SKE (node pool, node selector, affinity, topology spread) and records the value the replacement uses, or that it does not need it.

  - What: Review the 6 open critical settings of `module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_ecs_service.this` (aws_ecs_service) that the draft does not carry, and decide for each what the STACKIT target does: set it up by hand, or record that production does not need it (`task-definition`, `service-desired-count`, `twin-ecs-task-definition--cpu`, `twin-ecs-task-definition--memory`, `twin-ecs-task-definition--requires-compatibilities`, `twin-ecs-service--launch-type`)
  - Why ({why}): 6 critical settings of `module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_ecs_service.this`
  - Needs ({needs}): `task-definition` (record DEC-GEN-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883, no owner role stated); `service-desired-count` (record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883, Platform Architect); `twin-ecs-task-definition--cpu` (record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883, Platform Architect); `twin-ecs-task-definition--memory` (record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883, Platform Architect); `twin-ecs-task-definition--requires-compatibilities` (record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883, Platform Architect); `twin-ecs-service--launch-type` (record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883, Platform Architect)
  - Done when ({done when}): 6
  - Settings (6, the step is done when every one is closed):
    - `task-definition` (Platform Architect; decision register record DEC-GEN-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The ECS task definition: the containers a service runs, with their CPU and memory, environment, secrets and health checks.
      - Done when: A person translates the task definition into Kubernetes Deployments and Services on SKE (CPU and memory, environment, secrets and health checks become manifest fields); the rule names a Cloud Foundry push as the other way for a simple web app.
    - `service-desired-count` (Platform Architect; decision register record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. desired_count is the number of tasks ECS keeps running (ecs_service docs: 'Number of instances of the task definition to place and keep running'); on SKE it becomes Deployment replicas or a HorizontalPodAutoscaler floor. The draft writes it as the replicas of the Deployment in kubernetes/\<service>/manifests.yaml when it is a literal number (a HorizontalPodAutoscaler owns the count where the service has a scaling target and policy the draft translates; a number that is not visible statically is a REQUIRED_ token) — a person confirms the number.
      - Done when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
    - `twin-ecs-task-definition--cpu` (Platform Architect; decision register record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. aws_ecs_task_definition.cpu (workload compatibility) (a facet of the unit): the cpu units reserved for the whole task (required for Fargate). The draft turns it into the cpu request and limit of the container where it writes the manifest; the sizing against the node pools stays a decision.
      - Done when: A person sets the equivalent on the SKE workload (resource requests and limits, ephemeral storage limit, node pool placement) and records the value the replacement uses, or that it does not need it.
    - `twin-ecs-task-definition--memory` (Platform Architect; decision register record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. aws_ecs_task_definition.memory (workload compatibility) (a facet of the unit): the memory in MiB reserved for the whole task (required for Fargate). The draft turns it into the memory request and limit of the container where it writes the manifest; the sizing against the node pools stays a decision.
      - Done when: A person sets the equivalent on the SKE workload (resource requests and limits, ephemeral storage limit, node pool placement) and records the value the replacement uses, or that it does not need it.
    - `twin-ecs-task-definition--requires-compatibilities` (Platform Architect; decision register record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. aws_ecs_task_definition.requires_compatibilities (workload compatibility) (a facet of the unit): the launch types the task is validated against, EC2, EXTERNAL, FARGATE or MANAGED_INSTANCES (provider docs ecs_task_definition.html.markdown:237), which decides where the task can run and which task-level settings are allowed. Nothing of it is carried: the draft rewrites the task as Kubernetes manifests that name the containers, and stackit_ske_cluster has no argument for this task-level setting, so a person chooses the equivalent on the target. (knowledge-base evidence, not in this folder)
      - Done when: A person sets the equivalent on the SKE workload (resource requests and limits, ephemeral storage limit, node pool placement) and records the value the replacement uses, or that it does not need it.
    - `twin-ecs-service--launch-type` (Platform Architect; decision register record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CATALOG-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SE-865F7883)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. aws_ecs_service.launch_type (workload compatibility): the capacity the tasks run on, EC2, FARGATE or EXTERNAL (default EC2; provider docs ecs_service.html.markdown:251), which decides the isolation of a task, its sizing steps and where it can run. Nothing of it is carried: the draft rewrites the service as a Deployment that runs on an SKE node pool and stackit_ske_cluster has no argument for it (the unit names it NOT CARRIED), so a person decides the placement on the target. (knowledge-base evidence, not in this folder)
      - Done when: A person decides where the pods of the service run on SKE (node pool, node selector, affinity, topology spread) and records the value the replacement uses, or that it does not need it.
  - Risk ({risk}): 6 critical settings of `module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_ecs_service.this` open (the draft does not carry them): the migration of `module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_ecs_service.this`

      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. aws_ecs_task_definition.requires_compatibilities (workload compatibility) (a facet of the unit): the launch types the task is validated against, EC2, EXTERNAL, FARGATE or MANAGED_INSTANCES (provider docs ecs_task_definition.html.markdown:237), which decides where the task can run and which task-level settings are allowed. Nothing of it is carried: the draft rewrites the task as Kubernetes manifests that name the containers, and stackit_ske_cluster has no argument for this task-level setting, so a person chooses the equivalent on the target. (knowledge-base evidence, not in this folder)
      - Done when: A person sets the equivalent on the SKE workload (resource requests and limits, ephemeral storage limit, node pool placement) and records the value the replacement uses, or that it does not need it.
    - `twin-ecs-service--launch-type` (Platform Architect; decision register record DEC-OPS-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-UI-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SERVICE-THIS)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. aws_ecs_service.launch_type (workload compatibility): the capacity the tasks run on, EC2, FARGATE or EXTERNAL (default EC2; provider docs ecs_service.html.markdown:251), which decides the isolation of a task, its sizing steps and where it can run. Nothing of it is carried: the draft rewrites the service as a Deployment that runs on an SKE node pool and stackit_ske_cluster has no argument for it (the unit names it NOT CARRIED), so a person decides the placement on the target. (knowledge-base evidence, not in this folder)
      - Done when: A person decides where the pods of the service run on SKE (node pool, node selector, affinity, topology spread) and records the value the replacement uses, or that it does not need it.
  - Risk ({risk}): 6 critical settings of `module.retail_app_ecs.module.ui_service.aws_ecs_service.this` open (the draft does not carry them): the migration of `module.retail_app_ecs.module.ui_service.aws_ecs_service.this`

- State: executable: a person can start it now
- Risk: the assessment leaves {risk} is not complete until every one is closed; this step is the one step of them (`assurance/critical-semantics.json` says why each is open)
- Rollback (manual): the plan does not know how this step is undone: the person who carries it out records how

The steps, each by its name, with the resource that stands for {resource} above, its target objects, the steps it comes after besides those above and the lines that are its own (for a line above with a placeholder in braces, what stands for it):

- The review of the open settings of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq by the Security Architect (`MAN/semantic/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq/open-settings-security-architect`) — {resource} = `module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`
  - What: Review the 2 open critical settings of `module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq` (aws_mq_broker) that the draft does not carry, and decide for each what the STACKIT target does: set it up by hand, or record that production does not need it (`network-exposure`, `tc-user--password`)
  - Why ({why}): 2 critical settings of `module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`
  - Needs ({needs}): `network-exposure` (record DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED, Security Architect); `tc-user--password` (record DEC-SEC-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-MQ-BROKER-MQ, Security Architect)
  - Done when ({done when}): 2
  - Settings (2, the step is done when every one is closed):
    - `network-exposure` (Security Architect; decision register record DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The broker is either reachable from the public internet or only from private networks.
      - Done when: A person sets which networks may reach the STACKIT RabbitMQ instance with its access list (a list of CIDR networks); the draft sets none.
    - `tc-user--password` (Security Architect; decision register record DEC-SEC-MODULE-DEPENDENCIES-AWS-MQ-BROKER-MQ)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. aws_mq_broker.user.password: The broker user's login credential; a literal secret. Held critical: it carries or names a credential; the value is never copied and a person re-creates it on the target.
      - Done when: A person hands the clients the password of the generated target credential instead of the literal in the source.
  - Risk ({risk}): 2 critical settings of `module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq` open (the draft does not carry them): the migration of `module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`
- The review of the open settings of module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this by the Security Architect (`MAN/semantic/module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this/open-settings-security-architect`) — {resource} = `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this`
  - What: Review the 4 open critical settings of `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this` (aws_ecs_service) that the draft does not carry, and decide for each what the STACKIT target does: set it up by hand, or record that production does not need it (`td-container-definitions`, `td-container-secret-references`, `td-container-plaintext-secret-env`, `td-container-image-registry`)
  - Why ({why}): 4 critical settings of `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this`
  - Needs ({needs}): `td-container-definitions` (record DEC-SEC-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CARTS-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SERVICE-THIS, Security Architect); `td-container-secret-references` (record DEC-SEC-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CARTS-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SERVICE-THIS, Security Architect); `td-container-plaintext-secret-env` (record DEC-SEC-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CARTS-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SERVICE-THIS, Security Architect); `td-container-image-registry` (record DEC-SEC-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CARTS-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SERVICE-THIS, Security Architect)
  - Done when ({done when}): 4
  - Settings (4, the step is done when every one is closed):
    - `td-container-definitions` (Security Architect; decision register record DEC-SEC-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CARTS-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SERVICE-THIS)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. ecs_task_definition.container_definitions of the unit that runs the task. The containers of the task: images, commands, environment, secret references, ports, health checks, log configuration - the workload itself, and where its credentials are injected. Decide per container: the image and where it lives, command and entry point, environment, ports and health check, cpu/memory as requests and limits, and the log destination; the secret references and the plaintext secrets among the environment entries are read by the settings of the secret references and of the plaintext secrets, the registry by the setting of the image registry. The unit names module.dependencies.module.dynamodb_carts.aws_dynamodb_table.this (aws_dynamodb_table) through this setting (the target does not carry it: it is in the class redesign), so what the reference becomes is part of this setting.
      - Done when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
    - `td-container-secret-references` (Security Architect; decision register record DEC-SEC-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CARTS-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SERVICE-THIS)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. The task definition's container_definitions state secret references (the `secrets` list: name and valueFrom, "the full ARN of the AWS Secrets Manager secret or the full ARN of the parameter in the SSM Parameter Store", AWS ECS API reference, Secret). A person decides first which STACKIT Secrets Manager instance holds each referenced secret (stackit_secretsmanager_instance, pinned provider: name, acls, kms_key; the secret entries themselves have no Terraform resource in the pinned provider, they are created through the API or the portal, and their VALUES are never in the Terraform sources - a person copies them). Second, a person decides how the pods read them (the STACKIT Secrets Manager authenticates with Vault-compatible tokens and AppRole, docs.stackit.cloud/products/security/secrets-manager/; ECS injects valueFrom into environment variables when the container starts, and no STACKIT equivalent of that injection is stated on the documentation page checked 2026-10-06, so the Kubernetes Secret or the in-pod fetch is a design of its own).
      - Done when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
    - `td-container-plaintext-secret-env` (Security Architect; decision register record DEC-SEC-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CARTS-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SERVICE-THIS)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. An `environment` entry whose NAME looks like a credential (password, secret, token, api key, private key, credential) carries a non-empty plaintext value in the task definition (a name heuristic, not a proof: the value is never shown). Decide: whether the value is a secret; if it is, it must not be copied into a Kubernetes manifest or a Terraform variable default but stored in a STACKIT Secrets Manager entry and the plaintext copy in the AWS state and task definition rotated.
      - Done when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
    - `td-container-image-registry` (Security Architect; decision register record DEC-SEC-MODULE-RETAIL-APP-ECS-MODULE-CARTS-SERVICE-AWS-ECS-SERVICE-THIS)
      - Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit recognises the unit but only passes this setting to a person; it does not decide its value. A container image comes from Amazon ECR (a `.dkr.ecr.` or public.ecr.aws registry host) or needs `repositoryCredentials`. The images are not moved by the draft and a pod on STACKIT cannot assume the ECR pull rights of the ECS execution role. Decide: where the images live on STACKIT (the STACKIT Container Registry has a GA API, the Container Registry API specification, but no Terraform resource in pinned provider; a registry of your choice works too), how they are copied, and the pull secret the cluster uses.
      - Done when: A person confirms the value against the target, or the knowledge base gains a rule that decides this setting.
  - Risk ({risk}): 4 critical settings of `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this` open (the draft does not carry them): the migration of `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this`

- State: executable: a person can start it now

STACKIT has no log group or stream object: a group's name survives only as a label the shipper sends. Agents switch from the CloudWatch agent to a documented shipper (Grafana Alloy, OpenTelemetry Collector, Vector or Fluent Bit); log groups that keep their logs for the same number of days share one instance.
- Risk: the assessment classifies module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_container_insights as reconfigure: no step of this plan creates its target
- Risk: 1 critical setting of `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_container_insights` is open and part of this step: `tc-name` (`assurance/critical-semantics.json` says why each is open and what closes it)
- Risk: module.retail_app_ecs.aws_cloudwatch_log_group.ecs_container_insights reads 0 instances as the variables of the source stand, so the draft writes no resource for it; it exists in production only if the condition holds there (applies only if its count ((var.container_insights_setting == "enhanced" && var.lifecycle_events_enabled) ? 1 : 0) holds when OpenTofu plans — off with the defaults of var.container_insights_setting, var.lifecycle_events_enabled; it exists only if a variable file (-var-file), -var or TF_VAR_container_insights_setting/TF_VAR_lifecycle_events_enabled switches it on, and this scan saw no such value)

### `WP-29` — Secrets migration user retire (platform services)

- Owner: Security Architect
- Steps: 1 (1 requires a design decision)
- Affected resources: `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_db`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_opensearch`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.mq`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.orders_db`
- Waits for: `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-API-OPT-IN`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.orders_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`
- State: waits for decisions
- Done when: the block and the two outputs of stackit_secretsmanager_user.migration are gone from the draft, the state lists no such user, and the instance lists no user created for the migration

<details>
<summary>The 1 manual step of WP-29</summary>

#### The step "Remove the migration user stackit_secretsmanager_user.migration of the Secrets…" (`MAN/path/secrets-migration/retire-user`)

- Phase: Platform services
- What: Remove the migration user stackit_secretsmanager_user.migration of the Secrets Manager instance stackit_secretsmanager_instance.migrated_secrets once the last secret is written and read back: delete its block and its two outputs (stackit_secretsmanager_user_migration_password and stackit_secretsmanager_user_migration_username) from the draft and run tofu apply, so the provider deletes the user. Afterwards the state must not list it (tofu state list shows no stackit_secretsmanager_user.migration) and the instance must list no user of that name; give a workload that reads a secret a user of its own with write_enabled = false, or the instance's role binding.
- Why: The user has write access to the secrets engine of the whole instance, not to one secret, and STACKIT generated its password into the state in plain text (the sensitive output stackit_secretsmanager_user_migration_password). Setting write_enabled = false is no removal: the provider updates that argument in place and has no flag that disables a user, so the user and its password stay. The provider's delete returns without an error when the API answers 403 (terraform-provider-stackit 0.119.0, secretsmanager user resource), so a successful apply alone does not prove that the user is gone: look at the instance's users in the portal or with the ListUsers request. Take the entry out of the state by hand (tofu state rm stackit_secretsmanager_user.migration) only AFTER the user is deleted in the instance, for example when the instance is already gone: removing it from the state alone leaves a user with write access behind. The earlier versions of the state (a versioned backend, backups) still hold the password; once the user is deleted it opens nothing, and while it is kept those versions are secrets.
- Who: Security Architect
- When: Timing to be scheduled by the owner
- Needs: the draft, the state of the draft and the Secrets Manager instance's list of users
- Done when: the block and the two outputs of stackit_secretsmanager_user.migration are gone from the draft, the state lists no such user, and the instance lists no user created for the migration
- Source: `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_db`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_opensearch`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.mq`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.orders_db`
- After: the step "Read each migrated secret"
- State: not executable — requires a design decision — blocked by `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-API-OPT-IN`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.orders_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`; it also comes after the step "Read each migrated secret", a manual prerequisite; and, as the plan states: the risks listed below
- Risk: until this step is done a credential with write access to every secret of the instance exists and its password is in the state
- Risk: placed in the platform services phase after the step "Read each migrated secret", which it depends on; its own phase is security
- Rollback (manual): the plan does not know how this step is undone: the person who carries it out records how

</details>

### `WP-30` — Secrets read back (platform services)

- Owner: Security Architect
- Steps: 1 (1 requires a design decision)
- Affected resources: `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_db`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_opensearch`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.mq`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.orders_db`
- Waits for: `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-API-OPT-IN`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.orders_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`
- State: waits for decisions
- Done when: every migrated secret has a current version whose keys and values equal those of its document, or the difference is corrected with the write step and read back again

<details>
<summary>The 1 manual step of WP-30</summary>

#### The step "Read each migrated secret" (`MAN/path/secrets-migration/read-back`)

- Phase: Platform services
- What: Read each migrated secret (module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_db, module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_opensearch, module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.mq and module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.orders_db) back from the Secrets Manager instance stackit_secretsmanager_instance.migrated_secrets before the migration user is removed: with its login, send the ReadSecret request of the write step (GET /v1/\<instance id>/data/\<path>, the path the write step names) and check that the secret has a current version, that its keys are the keys of the document you supplied, and that its values equal the document's (compare a hash of each value on your side; print none).
- Why: The write steps stop on any answer but 200 and check that a current version exists, but they never compare what the instance holds with what the person supplied. The migration user is the login they write with: once it is removed a correction needs a new user, and the source secrets are what the later steps retire.
- Who: Security Architect
- When: Timing to be scheduled by the owner
- Needs: the documents named in the write steps' inputs (the STACKIT API step WriteSecret of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_db, the STACKIT API step WriteSecret of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_opensearch, the STACKIT API step WriteSecret of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.mq, the STACKIT API step WriteSecret of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.orders_db) and the login of stackit_secretsmanager_user.migration (its outputs stackit_secretsmanager_user_migration_password and stackit_secretsmanager_user_migration_username)
- Done when: every migrated secret has a current version whose keys and values equal those of its document, or the difference is corrected with the write step and read back again
- Source: `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_db`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_opensearch`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.mq`, `module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.orders_db`
- After: the STACKIT API step WriteSecret of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_db, the STACKIT API step WriteSecret of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_opensearch, the STACKIT API step WriteSecret of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.mq, the STACKIT API step WriteSecret of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.orders_db
- State: not executable — requires a design decision — blocked by `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-API-OPT-IN`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`, `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.module.orders_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`; and, as the plan states: the risks listed below
- Risk: a value printed while comparing is a leaked secret, so compare hashes and never the values themselves
- Risk: placed in the platform services phase after the STACKIT API step WriteSecret of module.retail_app_ecs.aws_secretsmanager_secret.catalog_db, which it depends on; its own phase is security
- Rollback (manual): the plan does not know how this step is undone: the person who carries it out records how

</details>

Target (after the draft is applied and the credential exists; the host and the management URL of the credential stackit_rabbitmq_credential.dependencies_mq are the outputs `dependencies_mq_rabbitmq_credential_host` and `dependencies_mq_rabbitmq_credential_management` of the draft (`tofu output -raw \<name>`), the password is the sensitive output the draft writes): `curl -u "\<target-user>:$TARGET_PASSWORD" "\<target-management-url>/api/overview"`. Check that the move host is in `parameters.sgw_acl` of the instance (remove it afterwards), reaches both management APIs, and that the clients of the broker are listed with the protocol, the library and the credentials each uses. Runbook section: `terraform/handover/broker-dependencies_mq/data-move.md#preflight`.

#### The data rehearsal of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (`MAN/data/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq/rehearsal`)

- Phase: Platform services
- What: Rehearse the import on a scratch virtual host of the instance: remove from a copy of the export the users and the permissions (the credential of the instance is the only user, and a hash is not a thing to carry), the policies with ha-mode or ha-sync-mode (classic mirroring is not supported in RabbitMQ 4, `rabbitmqadmin` v2 strips them with strip_cmq_keys_from_policies) and the queues you declare differently on the target (quorum instead of mirrored classic queues); create the virtual host (`curl -u "\<target-user>:$TARGET_PASSWORD" "\<target-management-url>/api/vhosts/rehearsal" -X PUT`), import the copy into it (the entries of the export name the virtual host they belong to, so the copy names the scratch virtual host in them: `curl -u "\<target-user>:$TARGET_PASSWORD" "\<target-management-url>/api/definitions/rehearsal" -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' -d @definitions-rehearsal.json`), list the result and point one copy of an application at the scratch host to run its tests. Delete the scratch virtual host afterwards (`-X DELETE`). Runbook section: `terraform/handover/broker-dependencies_mq/data-move.md#rehearsal`.
- Why: no STACKIT page describes the import of definitions exported from another broker and the permissions of the credential are not stated; only the rehearsal shows what the instance accepts and refuses

- Phase: Platform services
- What: Import the settled definitions into the instance before any publishing application moves, so that a message published to an exchange finds its queues (a message routed to no queue is dropped unless the publisher asks for it back): `curl -u "\<target-user>:$TARGET_PASSWORD" "\<target-management-url>/api/definitions" -X POST -H 'content-type: application/json' -d @definitions-target.json` (the file is the export without users and permissions, without the mirroring keys and with the changes the rehearsal settled), or the same virtual host by virtual host with `/api/definitions/\<vhost>`.
