#### The final copy of the data of module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this by dump and restore (`MAN/data/module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this/final-sync/dump-restore`)

- Phase: Cutover
- What: Dump inside the freeze: `mysqldump --host=\<source-host> --port=\<source-port> --user=\<source-user> --password --single-transaction --routines --triggers --events --set-gtid-purged=OFF --no-tablespaces --databases \<database-1> \<database-2> --result-file=\<dump-file>.sql`. `--set-gtid-purged=OFF` leaves the GTID statements out, which MariaDB does not take, and `--no-tablespaces` leaves out the CREATE TABLESPACE statements, which MariaDB does not support for InnoDB. The target instance must be empty before the load (the STACKIT how-to of the restore says so): load the dump with the mysql client, as the user of the instance's service key, into the empty instance: `mysql --host=\<target-host> --port=\<target-port> --user=\<target-user> --password --database=\<target-database> < \<dump-file>.sql 2> load.log`, and read every line of `load.log`. A DEFINER account that does not exist on the target, a collation the target does not know and a database the target does not have are the errors the checks name: each is settled by a person, never by skipping the object. Runbook section: `terraform/handover/database-dependencies_catalog_rds_this/data-move.md#final-sync-dump-restore`.
- Why: the dump is a consistent snapshot of the moment it starts; inside the freeze it is the final state of the source
- Who: Data Architect
- When: Timing to be scheduled by the owner
- Needs: the customer's statements in inputs.yaml: source.dataset.downtime; the maintenance window is not given: the plan states none and invents none; state the window, the blackout days and the longest downtime of one switch with ops.maintenance_window in the inputs file (inputs-template.yaml shows the empty answer), then run again
- Done when: the load finished, every database of the source is in the instance, and every error in load.log is settled
- Source: `module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this`
- After: the write freeze of module.dependencies.module.catalog_rds.aws_rds_cluster.this by dump and restore

### `WP-40` — Switch the workloads and the message producers and consumers to the target

- Owner: Platform Architect
- Steps: 7 (2 require a design decision, 5 are not executable as the plan stands)
- Affected resources: `module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.carts_service.aws_ecs_service.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.catalog_service.aws_ecs_service.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.checkout_service.aws_ecs_service.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.orders_service.aws_ecs_service.this`, `module.retail_app_ecs.module.ui_service.aws_ecs_service.this`
- Waits for: 18 decisions: `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-API-OPT-IN` and 17 more (every decision is in execution-plan.json, with the steps of the package that wait for it)
- State: not executable as the plan stands (5 of 7 manual steps)
- Done when: each step meets the condition stated with it below (7 different conditions).

<details>
<summary>The 7 manual steps of WP-40</summary>

#### The drain of the queue of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (`MAN/cutover/messaging-drain/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`)

- Phase: Cutover
- What: Drain the queues of the AWS broker module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (aws_mq_broker): its consumers go on reading them, with no producer sending to the broker any more, until every queue holds no message. Read the depth in CloudWatch: Amazon MQ for RabbitMQ publishes MessageCount (the sum of the ready and the unacknowledged messages) for the broker and, on 3.x brokers, for each queue (the dimensions Queue and VirtualHost; on 4.x brokers the figures of a queue come from the Prometheus endpoint), and Amazon MQ for ActiveMQ publishes QueueSize and InFlightCount for each queue and TotalMessageCount for the broker. On a RabbitMQ broker the management API gives the same figure for every queue: `curl -s -u "\<source-user>:$SOURCE_PASSWORD" "\<source-broker-endpoint>/api/queues" | jq '[.[] | (.messages_ready // 0) + (.messages_unacknowledged // 0)] | add'` is 0 when they are empty. An inactive durable topic subscriber (InactiveDurableTopicSubscribersCount) holds messages that only its own client can read: connect it to the AWS broker until it has read them. The plan does not read the names of the queues of the broker
- Why: the messages in module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq are drained, not copied: the definitions of a broker are exported and imported, its messages are not, a message still in a queue when the consumers move is read by nobody, and retiring the broker deletes it
- Who: Platform Architect
- When: after the producers moved, in the maintenance window, before the switch of the consumers
- Needs: the queues of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq and their consumers, the durable topic subscribers, the longest time the business lets the drain take, and what is done with a message no consumer can process
- Done when: every queue of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq reports no ready and no unacknowledged (in-flight) message with no producer sending to it, and no inactive durable subscriber holds a message
- Source: `module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`
- After: the traffic switch of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq
- State: not executable — requires a design decision — blocked by 17 decisions: `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq` and 16 more (decision group `BS-6d37814a1c92` of execution-plan.json); it also comes after the traffic switch of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq, a manual prerequisite; and, as the plan states: the risks listed below
- Risk: how long the drain takes is not computable: it depends on the depth of the queues of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq and on the rate of their consumers, which the source does not state
- Risk: a message that no consumer can process (a poison message) keeps its queue non-empty: the person who runs the drain decides what happens to it, and the plan reads no dead-letter queue of a broker
- Rollback (manual): the drain only reads: point the producers back at module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (their switch says how); what the consumers already processed is processed, and what the producers sent to the STACKIT instance in the meantime is not in AWS

#### The switch of the consumers of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (`MAN/cutover/messaging-consumers/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`)

- Phase: Cutover
- What: Switch the consumers of the broker module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq (aws_mq_broker) to the endpoint of the STACKIT instance (the host and port of a credential of stackit_rabbitmq_instance.dependencies_mq (stackit_rabbitmq_credential; the draft writes one for the instance: read its host and port after the apply)) once every queue of the AWS broker is empty: change the endpoint and the credentials in their configuration, start them against the STACKIT instance and stop the consumers that read the AWS broker
- Why: the consumers read the AWS broker module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq until their configuration points to the STACKIT instance; they move after its queues are empty so that no message stays behind
- Who: Platform Architect
- When: after the drain, in the maintenance window, before the checks of the verification
- Needs: the consumers of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq and where each one configures the endpoint and the credentials, and the values of the STACKIT instance (its credential: the broker users are not carried); the maintenance window is not given: the plan states none and invents none; state the window, the blackout days and the longest downtime of one switch with ops.maintenance_window in the inputs file (inputs-template.yaml shows the empty answer), then run again
- Done when: every consumer of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq reads from the STACKIT instance and none reads the AWS broker, and the AWS broker stays empty
- Source: `module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq`
- After: the drain of the queue of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq
- State: not executable — requires a design decision — blocked by 17 decisions: `DEC-GEN-CONVERT-EXPOSURE-WIDENED/module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq` and 16 more (decision group `BS-6d37814a1c92` of execution-plan.json); it also comes after the drain of the queue of module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq, a manual prerequisite; and, as the plan states: the risks listed below
- Risk: the plan does not read which applications consume from module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq or where they configure it: the owner of the application lists them
- Rollback (manual): change the consumers back to module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq: its queues are empty and it stays in place until the GO of the rollback decision step, and the producers are switched back as their own step says; what the producers sent to the STACKIT instance in the meantime is not in AWS. The way back:
  - (1) source retention: UNKNOWN — supplied by Migration Owner (module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq is not retired until the GO recorded at the rollback decision step; how long it and its final backup are kept after that is the owner's; closed when the date until which module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq is kept is recorded)
  - (2) rollback trigger: UNKNOWN — supplied by Migration Owner (the ROLLBACK recorded at the rollback decision step; the criteria that call it; closed when the criteria (which check fails, which metric passes which limit, by when) are recorded before the cutover)
  - (3) data written to the target: UNKNOWN — supplied by Platform Architect (what is written on STACKIT after the switch is not in AWS: it is copied back to module.dependencies.aws_mq_broker.mq before AWS serves again, or accepted as lost; closed when the choice is recorded before the cutover)

- Phase: Decommission
- What: Retire {what} in the AWS account after the GO: delete it the way it is managed (remove it from the source configuration and apply that, or delete it in the AWS console), once the resources that depend on it are retired (the steps it follows); a targeted destroy of the source configuration would also delete what depends on it

- Who: Identity Architect
- When: after the GO recorded at the rollback decision step
- Needs: the GO recorded at the rollback decision step
- Done when: {resource} no longer exists in the AWS account and its charges stop on the bill

- Risk: StackToIT performs none of this: it deletes nothing in any cloud, and no step of this plan automates the retirement
- Rollback (manual): re-create {resource} from the source configuration; what it held beyond its configuration is not restored
- After, for every step: the rollback decision step

## Not migrated

### The refusal of the setting `credentials-and-keys-settings` of module.dependencies.module.catalog_opensearch.aws_opensearch_domain.this (`REF/semantic/module.dependencies.module.catalog_opensearch.aws_opensearch_domain.this/credentials-and-keys-settings`)

- Source capability: the critical setting `credentials-and-keys-settings` of `module.dependencies.module.catalog_opensearch.aws_opensearch_domain.this`
- Reason: the target has no counterpart that carries this setting of the source (`TARGET_SETTING_UNSUPPORTED`)
- Missing on the target: counterpart that carries the setting `credentials-and-keys-settings` of aws_opensearch_domain (the knowledge base marks it UNSUPPORTED)
- Why: The mapping rule for this kind of unit found a setting the target has no counterpart for. The IAM master user, the master user name and password, the customer KMS key and the custom endpoint certificate, read where they are set whatever their value. STACKIT generates the OpenSearch credential (stackit_opensearch_credential username and password are computed), offers no customer KMS key for the instance and no custom endpoint certificate (pinned provider); a password is never carried.
